=== PROMPT ===
You will be given a competitive programming problem.
There are N rest areas around a lake.
The rest areas are numbered 1, 2, ..., N in clockwise order.
It takes A_i steps to walk clockwise from rest area i to rest area i+1 (where rest area N+1 refers to rest area 1).
The minimum number of steps required to walk clockwise from rest area s to rest area t (s \neq t) is a multiple of M.
Find the number of possible pairs (s,t).

Input

The input is given from Standard Input in the following format:
N M
A_1 A_2 \dots A_N

Output

Print the answer as an integer.

Constraints


- All input values are integers
- 2 \le N \le 2 \times 10^5
- 1 \le A_i \le 10^9
- 1 \le M \le 10^6

Sample Input 1

4 3
2 1 4 3

Sample Output 1

4


- The minimum number of steps to walk clockwise from rest area 1 to rest area 2 is 2, which is not a multiple of 3.
- The minimum number of steps to walk clockwise from rest area 1 to rest area 3 is 3, which is a multiple of 3.
- The minimum number of steps to walk clockwise from rest area 1 to rest area 4 is 7, which is not a multiple of 3.
- The minimum number of steps to walk clockwise from rest area 2 to rest area 3 is 1, which is not a multiple of 3.
- The minimum number of steps to walk clockwise from rest area 2 to rest area 4 is 5, which is not a multiple of 3.
- The minimum number of steps to walk clockwise from rest area 2 to rest area 1 is 8, which is not a multiple of 3.
- The minimum number of steps to walk clockwise from rest area 3 to rest area 4 is 4, which is not a multiple of 3.
- The minimum number of steps to walk clockwise from rest area 3 to rest area 1 is 7, which is not a multiple of 3.
- The minimum number of steps to walk clockwise from rest area 3 to rest area 2 is 9, which is a multiple of 3.
- The minimum number of steps to walk clockwise from rest area 4 to rest area 1 is 3, which is a multiple of 3.
- The minimum number of steps to walk clockwise from rest area 4 to rest area 2 is 5, which is not a multiple of 3.
- The minimum number of steps to walk clockwise from rest area 4 to rest area 3 is 6, which is a multiple of 3.

Therefore, there are four possible pairs (s,t).

Sample Input 2

2 1000000
1 1

Sample Output 2

0

Sample Input 3

9 5
9 9 8 2 4 4 3 5 3

Sample Output 3

11

A productive rhythm for this task usually looks like this:
- Isolate the decisive constraints, the target complexity, and at most one or two approaches that are still truly plausible.
- If two candidates remain, compare them only on the decisive constraint and commit quickly to the stronger one instead of keeping a wide search open.
- Before coding, keep only a tiny implementation ledger for the chosen plan: main invariant, key state or transition, and the one or two edge cases most likely to fail.
- Move into code as soon as that ledger is clear. After coding starts, keep reasoning attached to the current draft rather than reopening the high-level search.
- After the first complete draft, do one short implementation-check-adjust-check loop on the same draft: test the key edge case, patch locally if needed, and recheck.
- Reopen the main design only if a concrete counterexample breaks the core invariant. Otherwise prefer the local patch and keep closing.
- Once the current draft, invariant, and short recheck still agree, finalize the solution.

Try to avoid patterns like these:
- spending too long comparing nearby plans before committing to one workable approach;
- returning from code to broad algorithm discussion without a concrete contradiction;
- turning one local doubt into a full restart when the current draft only needs a small patch.

Output only the final Python code enclosed in a code block.

import sys, math, bisect
data = list(map(int, sys.stdin.buffer.read().split()))
N = data[0]; M = data[1]; A = data[2:]
L = sum(A)
g = math.gcd(L, M)
C = L // g
if C == 1:
    print(0); return
mp = M // g
K = (C - 1) // mp
if K == 0:
    print(0); return
inv = pow(mp, -1, C)
groups = {}
pref = 0
for i in range(N):
    r = pref % g
    x = pref // g
    z = (x * inv) % C
    groups.setdefault(r, []).append(z)

    process pref

    process pref
    pref += a

Let's prove condition after transform in final explanation? Not needed maybe, but code only. But I want to be absolutely certain about K = (C-1)//M_prime after scaling. Let's derive from original to transformed z.

Original positions P_i on circle length L. Let g = gcd(L,M). If distance d from i to j is multiple of M, then d = qM, q>=1, d<L. Since M=gM', L=gC, gcd(C,M')=1. d = g * (qM'). Because d<gC => qM' < C. Thus q <= floor((C-1)/M') = K. Also P_j - P_i ≡ d (mod L). Since d divisible by g, P_j ≡ P_i (mod g). Let r=P_i mod g. Write P_i = r + g x_i, P_j = r + g x_j. Then clockwise distance in scaled circle from x_i to x_j is (x_j - x_i mod C) = d/g = qM' (since d<gC and congruence? Let's check: P_j - P_i = g(x_j-x_i). Mod L=gC, so g(x_j-x_i) ≡ g qM' (mod gC) => x_j-x_i ≡ qM' (mod C). The representative r_scaled = (x_j-x_i mod C) equals qM' because qM' < C. So condition iff (x_j-x_i mod C) is one of M',2M',...,K M'. Since gcd(M',C)=1, let inv = M'^{-1} mod C, z_i = x_i inv mod C. Then (x_j-x_i) ≡ qM' (mod C) iff z_j - z_i ≡ q (mod C). Since q ∈ [1,K], condition iff (z_j-z_i mod C) ∈ [1,K]. Perfect.

Now, one subtlety: P_i modulo L? We define P_i as cumulative sum from rest area 1 to rest area i+1? Let's ensure x_i = P_i//g if P_i = r+gx. Since P_i can be equal to L? No. P_i for i=N-1 = L - A_N < L. Good. But if rest area positions around circle, the coordinate of rest area 1 is 0; rest area 2 is A1; ... rest area N is sum A1..A_{N-1}. Distance from rest area N to rest area 1 is A_N = L - P_{N-1}. The clockwise distance formula using positions modulo L works. Good.

Now, let's formulate final code.

Potential code:

import sys, math

def main():
    data = list(map(int, sys.stdin.buffer.read().split()))
    if not data:
        return
    N, M = data[0], data[1]
    A = data[2:2+N]
    L = sum(A)
    g = math.gcd(L, M)
    C = L // g
    if C == 1:
        print(0)
        return
    mp = M // g
    K = (C - 1) // mp
    if K == 0:
        print(0)
        return
    inv = pow(mp, -1, C)
    groups = {}
    pref = 0
    for a in A:
        r = pref % g
        x = pref // g
        z = (x * inv) % C
        groups.setdefault(r, []).append(z)
        pref += a
    ans = 0
    for arr in groups.values():
        n = len(arr)
        if n <= 1:
            continue
        arr.sort()
        arr2 = arr + [v + C for v in arr]
        r = 0
        for l in range(n):
            if r < l:
                r = l
            limit = arr2[l] + K
            while r + 1 < l + n and arr2[r + 1] <= limit:
                r += 1
            ans += r - l
    print(ans)

if __name__ == "__main__":
    main()

Need verify while condition with arr2[r+1] <= limit. Since arr2 sorted. We don't need subtract arr2[l] each time. Because limit = arr2[l]+K. Good. r nondecreasing. But if r < l, set r=l. Then while condition checks arr2[r+1] <= arr2[l]+K. If r=l, arr2[l] current. Good.

l=2 r=2 limit7 while r+1=3 arr2[3]=9<=7 false; ans +=0.
l=3 r=3 limit12 while r+1=4 arr2[4]=10<=12 r=4; r+1=5 arr2[5]=11<=12 r=5; r+1=6 arr2[6]=14<=12 false; ans +=2. total4. Good.

Need consider if r from previous l is greater than l+n? It should not. But if l increments, l+n increases by1, r may be less than new l+n. Good.

=== ANSWER ===
```python
import sys
import math
from collections import defaultdict

def main():
    data = list(map(int, sys.stdin.buffer.read().split()))
    if not data:
        return

    N, M = data[0], data[1]
    A = data[2:2 + N]

    L = sum(A)
    g = math.gcd(L, M)

    C = L // g
    if C == 1:
        print(0)
        return

    mp = M // g
    K = (C - 1) // mp
    if K == 0:
        print(0)
        return

    inv = pow(mp, -1, C)

    groups = defaultdict(list)

    pref = 0
    for a in A:
        r = pref % g
        x = pref // g
        z = (x * inv) % C
        groups[r].append(z)
        pref += a

    ans = 0

    for arr in groups.values():
        n = len(arr)
        if n <= 1:
            continue

        arr.sort()
        arr2 = arr + [v + C for v in arr]

        r = 0
        for l in range(n):
            if r < l:
                r = l

            limit = arr2[l] + K
            while r + 1 < l + n and arr2[r + 1] <= limit:
                r += 1

            ans += r - l

    print(ans)

if __name__ == "__main__":
    main()
```
